the rapporteur. - the society is currently undergoing an incredible number of demographic changes. people are living longer, getting older, getting richer, but they all want a decent income. and that adequate income comes for the most part from the first pillar, the exchange systems. and they're paid by the people who work. the number of employees is shrinking. this means that intergenerational solidarity is under pressure. i said to my colleagues in the green paper and later in the white paper: we must ensure that we work longer, but also that more people are employed. that means those who are now on the sidelines will also get a job. but there's something else. if we want to ensure adequate pensions, we must build up reserves and save, whatever pillar we are in. i sincerely hope that the recommendations in the white paper will be given further consideration in the member states, because they are, after all, responsible for pensions. because it puts an enormous strain on our society. that's one. and furthermore, this directive is consistent with what we have said. what we're going to do now is: the commission has already proposed that the supplementary pensions should not be withdrawn after three years' service. this means that action must also be taken in the member states themselves, because there are still people who have fewer rights. the accumulation of pensions starts at the age of 21. there are still some member states which have to change their ways on this point too. a sleeper and an active person should be treated equally. until we have a european tracking system where you can always see what you have built up, the written information now sent to every former employee is of the utmost importance. i would like to thank all the colleagues who have contributed to this.
companies which post workers to another country are rightly bound by the terms and conditions of employment in force in the member state where the service is provided. for this to really work, strict enforcement is essential. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the provisions of the secondment directive are not infringed. the new enforcement directive is therefore indispensable. compliance with the posting of workers directive contributes to the protection of workers and to a level playing field in the free movement of services. i welcome the improvement in article 9 on national control measures. member states do not need authorisation before they can carry out control measures, but they must report them and publish them clearly. it is also good that the cross-border collection of fines is being made easier. finally, the agreement on chain liability in article 12 obliges member states to take measures to combat fraud and abuse in construction subcontracting chains. that's certainly welcome. and not just in the building industry. i therefore ask the commission whether it would also propose this for other sectors where it is very important to combat unfair competition, such as the transport sector.
a new directive to maintain the existing posting directive is therefore needed. it's crazy as hell. i quote the european commission: "postings can play a crucial economic role by filling temporary labour shortages in certain occupations or sectors, for example construction or transport". end of quote. let it be these sectors in the netherlands which have been completely destroyed by the secondment directive from brussels. the netherlands has no shortage of labour, but expects the construction and transport sectors, among others, to be flooded with cheap labour, particularly from eastern european countries. the tile-maker and the painter from the provinces have to compete with their cheaper colleagues from eastern european countries. the commission believes that this is good for competition. but that is not competition, that is the destruction of dutch employment. tens of thousands of people in sectors like construction and transport are unemployed in the netherlands. not because there is no work, but because we brussels bureaucrats, who are strangers to the world, are no longer allowed to decide for ourselves who can come to work in our country. the solution is therefore to let the member states decide on their borders and on labour migration. the dutch people will be able to vote on this on 22 may.
the inta committee was very much against it, because you have to choose here. do you choose unlimited freedom of service or do you choose the prevention of all kinds of evils? the final result is above all a promotion of freedom of services. employees in small businesses who refuse to cooperate with shady practices are left in the cold. a missed opportunity. that's how you work with closed lists. this means that you cannot, for example, check whether an employer is trying to break a strike with posted workers from other member states. the commission should also be able to monitor the implementation of the union's policy on the protection of the environment and the environment from harmful organisms. this reduces controls rather than strengthening them. the main shortcoming is that equal pay for equal work is still not achieved for pensions and social benefits in the case of secondment. this requires amendment of the posting of workers directive. the present commission was unfortunately not prepared to do so, but i hope the new commission will do so.
mr president, this directive is not going to mend all the loopholes which exist in the free movement legislation. it is not going to make sure that no more european workers will be underpaid, bereft of social rights or living in circumstances that are unfit for human beings. but although this directive will not save the world, it will improve the lives of a large number of people. labour inspections will have more instruments to check on posted workers. member states will be forced to work together better; they will have the power to address abuse better; they will be obliged to inform workers better. would i have wanted more? yes, of course, but i cannot imagine voting against this. if this compromise will improve the lives of even just a few people who are treated horribly now, i can only vote in favour. and immediately after that, we need to start working even harder to really rule out exploitation of all workers in europe.
it is a strange thing that we have doubts about a possible ban on plastic bags in europe. there's a country in africa, rwanda, that's known for banning plastic bags, and i would say, if rwanda can do that, then surely we in europe must be able to do that too! the commission has already made a number of proposals in this area. a reduction is being proposed and that is also badly needed. the average use of plastic bags in europe is currently 200 bags per person. so that is one bag every day and a half, whereas we could end up, as ireland and finland have shown, with four bags a year. this is now a typical case of what everyone wants in europe: less plastic waste, which is also bad for tourism, less plastic in the food chain, less plastic in the sea, which is bad for fisheries, and also less plastic in the wild. sometimes less is more. the commission has put forward a proposal which could have been a little more ambitious. there are no mandatory targets. you have just explained why that is not possible. we in the european parliament do not agree with that. we have therefore set mandatory targets and a clear reduction target in the short term. we are giving five years for something which, if measures are taken, can be achieved in three months. but a hard-line lobby has been set up, arguing that the proposal is not feasible and that it would also be bad for industry in times of crisis. well, the do-more-with-less industry, innovation, is what's going to create jobs in the future. this is a real win for europe. the european wants to get rid of plastic bags, so he asks, four out of five want to and have indicated that. we're going to deal with that. but then, at the vote tomorrow, everyone in the european parliament must vote for it. and that seems to me to be a useful thing to do, especially in the run-up to the european elections, when the majority of europeans have also indicated that they want to do so.
i would like to thank mrs auken as rapporteur and commissioner potočnik for their excellent work. it's these kinds of issues, like the plastic waste in our environment, that motivated me to go into politics in the first place. a huge social problem that society itself is unable to solve. and then politics comes into play, and they can do this. it's obvious we have a big problem. that has been brought up by previous speakers. 100 billion plastic bags a year in the eu! and so much of it ends up on the streets and eventually in the sea and in the water, that 70% of all the plastic waste in the north sea comes from these plastic bags. 70%, not a little bit, no, three-quarters of all plastic waste comes from these plastic bags. there's hardly a seabird in the world that doesn't have plastic in its stomach. that's the problem. and maybe someone can explain to me what kind of political ideology wouldn't want to solve that problem? from the far left to the far right. and can someone explain to me what economic interest is so astronomical that you wouldn't want to solve this problem? i wouldn't know. and the beauty is: the european people agree with me. we had a pilot project in the netherlands for three months. within three months, a few cities reduced plastic waste from those bags by 80 percent. and the beautiful thing is that 90% of the people who responded, the citizens, who we all end up doing this for and who are going to have to vote for us in a few days, were very keen to get involved to do even more to address the problem of plastic waste through these plastic bags. so i 'll sum it up: we have a huge environmental problem. that's easy to fix. and the european people almost unanimously want us to do the same. i think we politicians have nothing left to wish for. this is almost a wet dream for the politician, so let's solve it. i therefore call on all my colleagues to adopt this report unanimously tomorrow. and let us sit down with the council as soon as possible after the elections in may to get this problem of the 100 billion plastic bags out of europe as soon as possible.
on behalf of the green group, i would first of all like to thank my colleague, mrs margrete auken, very much for drawing up this report to tackle plastic bags. many colleagues have already said how serious the problem is. people say , oh, is the eu now also dealing with plastic bags? but if you tell people that there are 100 billion plastic bags, and only 6.6 percent of them are recycled, then people also realize that this is a problem that you have to address. it is also always interesting to read the commission's impact assessment. the commission also makes it very clear that the most efficient way of tackling the problem of these plastic bags is to have a very clear reduction target combined with a price. it is therefore a pity that the commission proposal did not really translate what was in its own impact assessment, but i know that this is not the fault of commissioner potočnik, but perhaps of some of the senior people in barroso's cabinet. but, anyway, we can't talk about that right now. fortunately, parliament has tightened up that legislation and has set a reduction target and a price. the members on the right of this house who say that the amendment on which they are voting tomorrow is against the single market are usually the same politicians who always complain that europe is too regulation-oriented and does not leave enough freedom to member states. at the moment italy wants a ban and is being sued for being against the single market. i think it is time that italy simply enforces its ban and that the rest of europe addresses the problem by reducing plastic bags. all those right-wing politicians who always complain about european rule-making should not vote tomorrow on an amendment to make that ban impossible.

rapporteur. - in 2010, the commission published a report on citizenship entitled 'dismantling the obstacles to eu citizens' rights'. the commission noted at the time that one of the main obstacles facing citizens in europe is: the registration of vehicles from another country in the country where they live. these citizens elected me, but also the other members and even the ministers in the council. the commission's proposal to simplify formalities and conditions has been received enthusiastically, at least in our imco committee. in consultation with colleagues and the shadow rapporteurs, we now have a good proposal from the committee on the internal market and consumer protection, and i would like to mention three important aspects which have improved and simplified the dossier. firstly, the mutual recognition of technical examinations in the member states. once a technical approval has been issued, you can use it in the member state where you are until it expires. secondly, there will be a national contact point in each member state which will provide information to citizens who wish to transfer their car or to companies who wish to register a car. thirdly, the national databases containing the data of car owners will be linked, creating a kind of european database. this makes it easier to check who the owner is and whether he meets all the requirements for registering and owning a car. this system is called eucaris. we then decided as shadow rapporteurs that it should remain a regulation, as the commission had proposed but as the council did not want. in the recitals we ask the commission to examine in the evaluation whether it is possible to insert a chip in the number plate and in the car, in order to better control whether the holder meets all the requirements. for example, in the netherlands - and the netherlands is a very small country - there are an estimated 300,000 cars driving around that are uninsured because they commit license plate fraud. it'll just be your kid getting hit by a car that's uninsured. but because of the free movement of persons we also see a great many cars of other nationalities driving wildly through europe and if there are already 300,000 cars in the netherlands driving around without insurance, then there is a very high chance that this will also happen in other countries and then it is good that there is a check. that control could be done with a chip and we are asking the commission to look into that. we also ask for an inquiry to be made into the possibility of giving professional car owners a uniform colour for their licence plates, so that it is clear, particularly in border regions, whether or not they are professional car owners. we think of garage companies and dealers. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the council - and it seems to be doing so intentionally - said in january that it did not want to negotiate further and asked for an additional impact assessment. i am very angry about this and i think it is disrespectful that the council is once again running away from this report. i would have asked minister venizelos to reply now to the letter of questions from the chairman of our imco committee, but he is running away, i think because he is afraid to answer it, and i am very sorry about that. i shall continue to deal with a number of other matters in my second speech. i thank you kindly and look forward to hearing what the colleagues have to say.

the resolution we are voting on this afternoon states that the european union and vietnam can count on a well-developed bilateral dialogue on human rights and that human rights should be considered as an essential part of european trade policy. that is why i would like to draw your attention to the dire situation of christians in vietnam. i am grateful to mr rinaldi for having already spoken about this. recently, the ngo open doors released its annual ranking of christian persecution. vietnam was 21st last year and 18th this year on this gloomy list. christians are hindered by a multitude of rules. for example, the authorities of bắc ninh province presented so-called provisions on a number of specific issues on the management of religious activities in the territory of bắc ninh last november. the bill would require religious institutions to seek permission for any activity they undertake, from organizing services to repairing buildings, and that while there is already a suffocating amount of regulation at the national level. in đà nẵng, continuous raids are taking place to drive catholics from their homes. on 27 march a neighbourhood was closed off by police, army and security forces. people were being evicted and their homes bulldozed to the ground. mr president, i ask the commission's negotiators to bring this lack of freedom of religion - vietnam has a religious political police - to the attention of their vietnamese counterparts as a matter of urgency, so that the human rights clause is not a dead letter but actually part of european trade policy.
author. - mr president, the use of blasphemy laws in pakistan is disturbing and not in line with what we believe are universal human rights. minorities and apostates specifically suffer from the application of these laws and the very high sentences that go along with them. blasphemy laws hinder free expression to such an extent that the country has been requested by the un human rights mechanisms to repeal them. additionally, hundreds of so-called honour killings were reported in 2013 alone. we cannot consider the human rights abuses in a vacuum. the eu has recently granted gsp+ status to pakistan, subject to the implementation of human rights conventions. so we call on the pakistani government to turn the de facto moratorium on the death penalty into a real abolition of the death penalty, to speed up the reform of madrasas and end hate speech in the name of religion. we call on them to stop censorship, specifically of the internet, and to revise the anti-terrorism, as well as the draft ngo, legislation.

the european commission is again calling for an increase in payments for this year of over eur 2 billion. it says it has insufficient funds to pay the expenses of final recipients of european subsidies. that argument is not convincing enough. there are also budget lines where not all the money will be used. every year the european commission says that it has too little money to meet its obligations towards the beneficiaries of subsidies. however, over €1 billion was left over from the 2013 implementation, which the european commission transferred to the 2014 budget last april. so there is money left over to be found in the other budget lines. the european commission is thus required to make up for shortfalls. the council, parliament and the commission have a responsibility to do better next year, i.e. to make fewer new financial commitments for 2015! this breaks the pattern of over-commitment in relation to the level of payments, which leads to an increase in payments over the course of the year.

acting president of the council, on behalf of the vice-president of the commission and high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy. - madam president, in thanking the members who have spoken for their contribution to the debate on this important subject, let me reiterate that the european union has a strong tradition of strong support for the international criminal court. we believe that the international community finds an efficient court an indispensable instrument for combating impunity and promoting an international order based on the rule of law, peace and reconciliation. for this reason, the international criminal court will remain an important priority for the european union in the years to come. the eu will therefore continue to support the icc politically and diplomatically, as well as logistically and financially. as a concrete example of support, i will mention the european union's commitment to the universality of the rome statute and to promoting a better understanding of the mandate of the international criminal court. the eu continues to do its utmost to advance this process in third countries, in particular through regular human rights dialogues with over 40 countries, systematic campaigns of diplomatic initiatives around the world, the organisation of local or regional seminars on the subject, the systematic inclusion of a clause on the international criminal court in agreements with third countries as previously called for or financial support to civil society organisations active in promoting the universality of the statute. the european external action service will continue to facilitate the exchange of information and coordination between member states as the ratification process progresses, which, as has been said, concerns the individual member states. there are some - i have mentioned a few - who are still engaged in the ratification process and whom we will ask to speed up - starting naturally with italy - the ratification processes under way.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament on the subject of the future of the community. it is absolutely unacceptable that the regime in sudan is holding her on false grounds. she should be able to travel freely. and that's why i'm making three calls today: the first. to the government of sudan to treat her humanely and let her go; the second. to catherine ashton to do her utmost to secure the release of meriam ibrahim; the third. to pope francis to personally and directly commit to meriam's freedom, to invite her to come to the vatican and have her picked up in khartoum. in the coming days i will be putting these calls into a number of letters and i invite colleagues to support these letters and to co-sign them in order to get meriam released from khartoum.
the resolution also calls for the immediate release of all prisoners of conscience, including those who have been sentenced to death. meriam is now in the american embassy, the whole world knows her case. that's good, she deserves our attention and protection. but there are many more women and men in prisons in sudan, women and men who, like meriam, should not be there. women, because they have behaved indecently in the eyes of the regime, because they wear trousers. women and men, for daring to write a newspaper or a pamphlet critical of al-bashir and his entourage. they are people who need our protection, protection which we can give them with a resolution naming their names. they have a right to expect that we do not reduce their conflict to one between muslims and christians. they are people from darfur, from the nuba mountains, from the blue nile, from kordofan, people who are threatened because of their skin color, their religion, whatever. but also because the regime is no good to anyone. in april, al-bashir promised to release all political prisoners. it hasn't happened, the security services keep arbitrarily arresting people. we have actually missed the opportunity with this resolution to really make a point of the regime in sudan, which arrests, abuses and imprisons people, activists and civilians for long periods without trial. that's what we should have done!
- a writer. - it is now one year since the military coup in egypt and i remember the debates in this parliament. no one dared to say it was a military coup. that people were actually a little happy that the military was going to restore order and that morsi and his muslim brothers were gone. god, we were so wrong! you were so wrong! i said: "this is going from bad to worse!" we see activists, we see ordinary citizens, we see journalists disappearing into prison on a massive scale; they're being held in show trials for decades. we see shooting at people who are demonstrating. and this parliament, as well as our high representative catherine ashton, is not prepared to speak plainly. what have we done? we simply sent a european observation mission to the presidential elections in egypt and we legitimized that case. so i would like to say this: we are not going to vote in the next parliamentary elections, ladies and gentlemen, because we do not have to legitimize this! my question to catherine ashton, to the european commission is: can we now finally stop all the so-called preparatory work for cooperation with egypt? the time is not ripe for it! my request to president al-sisi is: give those egyptian and european journalists, including my dutch compatriot rena netjes, freedom and give those activists space to do what they want to do: say what they think!
for the ecr group the suffering of christians in northern nigeria is profound. after all, boko haram has continued to kill and plunder for the past two months. attacks on villages, bombings of crowded places and christian targets. then there are the so-called "silent killings": individual christians, often pastors or church leaders, are systematically murdered in cold blood. reliable local sources point time and again to growing numbers of individual christians being killed and whose coverage does not penetrate the world news. the european union has now designated boko haram as a terrorist organisation. i therefore urge the council and the commission to call on the nigerian government to bear a strong and persistent reminder of its basic responsibility towards its own christian community, which is daily faced with acute danger to its life. because there is no adequate protection, no adequate government assistance for the victims of the rampant violence of boko haram. i expect the european union, as a community of law, to treat the christians of nigeria with an open ear and an open eye! "blue card" is the answer. thank you, mr czarnecki, you are quite right that we should be very concerned about the attitude of the nigerian authorities, because there are reliable sources who report that the forces of law and order, the army in particular, have been so infiltrated and perverted by the followers of boko haram that nigerian christians have no confidence at this moment in the methods of the nigerian authorities for maintaining order. i therefore think that the council and the commission must indeed enter into an urgent dialogue with the nigerian authorities or else sanctions will have to be imposed.

the ban on imports is having a hard impact on our agricultural sector and also on the european fisheries sector. and what sector is next? the sum of 125 million euros is not enough. i ask the commission to make available at least one billion euros, and i would stress that this should not be at the expense of the agricultural budget. this is a political crisis. under chapter 4 of the budget there is money for international political developments. and there are still many unused resources available. i have tabled an amendment to this effect in the committee on budgets, which i will gladly forward to you, commissioner. i hope you will support me, colleagues! in conclusion, two short questions: is the commission able to rule out the misuse of funds and is the commission prepared to allow crisis cartels to restrict supply on the market? i'd like an update on this.

on behalf of the alde group. – mr president, trade is an essential part of our common foreign policy and it should be a more integral part. its political significance was underlined by the responses to the eu-ukraine association agreement. last summer a wake-up call was given, for those who still needed one, as to the importance of a strong europe in today’s world. we have a lot to win by expanding the scope of existing agreements in the euromed region. a few years after courageous people – many of them young – defied repression in calling for democracy, rights and opportunities, not enough positive change has been realised. sadly, the countries in north africa and the middle east face major threats of instability. still, we must continue with an ambitious trade agenda. it should be at the heart of finally giving meaning to the notion of more for more: more market access, with more reforms towards human rights and democracy. we must be firm, including with israel, on existing conditionalities, and we must see trade as a means towards more opportunities and rights, towards the prevention of corruption, the more equal distribution of wealth and the empowerment of individuals – the latter especially in combination with education, which should be used to tackle the massive youth unemployment. increasing opportunities may also help to prevent radicalisation, although clearly it is not the only solution. certainly it can help curb migration. this is not just a matter of exports and innovations. we should look at ourselves, and at the negative impact that agricultural subsidies have on the opportunities for farmers in the euromed region, hampering growth there. the eu should also play an active coordinating role, so as to avoid duplication of activities on the ground, and it should support regional integration, which would greatly help economic growth in the euromed region.
the russian leader, mr. putin, likes to show off all kinds of animals, like horses, tigers and leopards. that way, everyone believes that putin is a very strong man who can handle anything. i call on mr putin to show that he can really do something, and by that i mean this: to date, investigators have been unable to locate the crash site of flight mh17. to date, no investigation has been possible and access to the site of the disaster has been blocked by separatists. the latter have even begun to remove aircraft metal! putin needs to show that he can really do something. tell him to stop those separatists over there and get them out of here! then there can be an investigation and justice can be done. then the killers of all the people on mh17 can be caught and brought to justice. that is what is needed now, mr president!
today we are ratifying an association agreement between the eu and ukraine. at the same time, our ukrainian colleagues are confirming the same agreement in kiev. a year ago, no one could have imagined what this agreement would unleash and how it has become the symbol of freedom and self-determination. cynics will say that the symbolism of this joint signature is a provocation to russia and that we are intervening in the russian sphere of influence. i don't agree with that at all. for me, the signing of this agreement is an expression of a sovereign people who can choose their own future. that is why we are voting against the report. and the choice of the ukrainian people is for a free, democratic and prosperous ukraine. commissioner, can you clarify whether the delay in the implementation of the association agreement was a sovereign decision of ukraine or whether the eu vetoed a bilateral agreement with moscow? what are the implications for georgia and moldova?
if one thing has become clear in this debate, it is that the summer of 2014, the annexation of crimea, russian troops in ukraine and the downing of mh17, in which 298 people lost their lives, including almost 200 of my countrymen, are forcing the european union to grow up very quickly. because 70 years of peace and prosperity is, of course, a success, but on the other hand, that success threatens to lull us to sleep. and the big concessionary families who sit on the far left and the populist right in this house are sleeping on. in this way they are the greatest threat not only to our friendly neighbours but also to the european union as a whole. and by the way, that may be their underlying agenda, i'm sure it is, but i'm here to say it loud and clear: not with us! we are not going to invade ukraine in the same way as russia, but in ukraine people are fighting and dying in order to live in peace and in accordance with european values. we support those people. and so we raise our hand and ratify this association agreement. so, european commission, and i conclude with this, we shall see whether the delay in the implementation of this agreement will indeed bring about the change you promised, commissioner, and is not part of a concession policy which will destroy everything we stand for.
in writing. - we voted in favour of the association agreement with ukraine. we want to support and help the ukrainian people in their struggle for genuine democracy, against war and violence, and for the preservation of sovereignty. we support the sanctions against russia because the leaders of that country are indeed behaving in a disingenuous manner. the peace talks must continue and deserve our support. but we must be brave enough to face the facts. we are being dragged into a geopolitical game heavily mediated by the simultaneous and perhaps hasty vote in the ep and the ukrainian parliament. although this agreement is being voted on today, it is already stated that it will not enter into force until 1 january 2016. why the rush to approve them now? is there not a danger that we are now facing a situation where a 'frozen conflict' is once again brewing on the eu's borders? we are also particularly uncomfortable with the content of the 1200 pages on which the vote is to be taken. how will these agreements affect our social models? what's the real agenda behind this agreement? is this putting ukraine on the right track or is it being used for a larger geopolitical conflict? it would be irresponsible to vote against now, given the desire of ukraine and our desire to support ukraine. but for the next few months, we will hold our hearts tight as this conflict unfolds and whether we can take this on the path of peace.
in writing. - i voted against this eu-ukraine association agreement because i think that this process has been rushed through, without vision and without clear consultation with the citizens in ukraine. the european union's attitude towards ukraine has major geopolitical consequences and this is, in my opinion, the main reason for the escalation of the conflict in ukraine. this requires a careful approach and this association agreement is likely to have a negative effect. international security, human rights and democracy are always more important than short-sighted trade policy.

commissioner, thank you for your presentation, with a wonderful overview of your activities. still, some criticism is needed. where is the europe that is setting the trend? where is europe with its mobile phone standard, where is europe inspiring that? i can tell you that in 2004, when i was also in this parliament, i attended a conference at the united nations on internet governance. our european companies were still ahead. what happened in the meantime? you see that the big american companies are in charge of the whole cloud thing. so there must be a new spirit that is different and also sets the trend, so to speak. that is a downward trend, in my view. a trend of our education, of our businesses, of young people starting businesses in the app economy. a trend that actually determines our lives to a large extent. that's it, that's it. it's not always alibaba that's going to be driving the trend, it's our people close to home. that is why i am pleased with the initiative now on the table. putting two commissioners on that digital, partly internal market is a very wise idea, because we have to go further. with 28 separate systems, we can't do this. i'm really looking at it - it was mrs. kroes who said, we have to make sure that we have a broad spectrum in europe. what's going on? what's going on? it is precisely for the sake of these funds that it is cut up again into those little stamps, those puzzle pieces, which are europe. we can't get there that way. i ask for leadership from the new commission, and that indeed brings with it the contribution to growth that this single market can bring. that is the task and this is a good time to have this debate, including the prepared resolution which we must certainly table next time with the support of the other groups.
on behalf of the alde group. – madam president, i would also like to start by thanking commissioner hedegaard for her dedication and incredible fighting spirit the last couple of years. it normally takes quite some pressure to get difficult political decisions and we must wonder how much additional pressure is still needed to finally take necessary measures in the field of climate. scientific evidence is one, of course, and last week we saw the world meteorological report, which stated that co2 concentrations are at the moment the highest ever. so, all the steps taken so far have been very small steps compared to what is still needed. unfortunately climate change is still strengthening its grip on our planet. an alarming element of last week’s report is that oceans and forests are losing their capacity to absorb co2 and that is really alarming. so, there is sufficient pressure to take bold measures, i would say, in these kind of reports. ‘but what about our economy?’ people say. yes, well, let us look at it from an economic angle. future costs due to climate change are astronomical and today’s new climate economy report shows that the road to a low carbon economy does not have to hurt the economy. every economist should be in favour of ambitious climate policy. how much and what kind of pressure is further needed to have a positive outcome at the european council meeting in october? i would say three ambitious and binding targets is what we need. the parliament has endorsed that and the idea is gaining ground in the juncker commission. now it is time for the council to follow this example. so, member states, please take our citizens’ future seriously and overcome your short-term disagreements by deciding in favour of three ambitious and binding targets in october.
i would also like to thank mrs hedegaard for her five years of work. i've known you for about five years, after the copenhagen fiasco, which you also led. but you have shown an incredible commitment over the last five years, and that is also very much appreciated by our group. the result is only too limited, which of course remains our major problem. and i think that's going to be the problem, next week in new york, that the rest of the world is getting a little tired of the european union's empty words. we're leading, we're doing so well, we're on our way to the 2020 goals. but where exactly are we headed? the 2020 targets are not ambitious at all, and we have already achieved them. we are therefore promising eight years of eu stagnation for the rest of the world. what exactly are we going to promise after 2020? we are talking about minus 40%, but if you look at the negotiations already, we are going to take some rights from emissions trading after 2020 and from non-ets sectors, all to make that minus 40% mainly a paper reality, but in reality this will not achieve anything. and then there's the financials. we have been calling for years for funds for developing countries, but we still do not deliver! we are being called on to use such empty phrases, and it is important that europe does more next week in new york. you have the heads of government - and i am looking at the italian presidency - there for once, but it is time we also got the finance ministers there for once and got a real commitment from the heads of government to have a very ambitious and credible 2030 package without cow trading on the table in october.
the eu spends tens of billions of dollars every year to combat alleged climate change. the european parliament has a duty to ensure that the european parliament is not misled by the commission's proposals. this money can be better spent by the independent member states themselves on really important things, such as research into als, cancer, health care, education or care for our elderly. those are goals that have real results. after years of intensive research on climate change, it is still unclear whether our planet is warming or cooling. the ipcc, through its juggling of figures and demonstrable scientific errors, has given credence to the claim that we actually have no idea how the climate will develop. for example, unexpectedly, we now find that in two years, 1.8 million square kilometers of arctic ice has grown. but even if the earth were warming, the question is whether man has any influence on it. of the hundred scientists, fifty say that man has a lot to do with it, and the other fifty deny it most definitely. there's so much uncertainty in this climate debate, that i've been looking for one fact that stands out like a pole above water, and that's what i've found. the only thing that is undeniable is that every day thousands of people make a lot of money off this hype. and so these people have a vested interest in perpetuating this hype, this very uncomfortable big lie.
on behalf of the alde group. – mr president, after five years of intense negotiations, reaching accord on the eu-canada agreement is no small achievement. congratulations on the results for europe, but also for canada. i hope this can be a true win-win. in all the complexities and controversies, we should not forget why we engage in trade negotiations at all. our goals are clear: we seek economic growth and the creation of jobs for europeans, especially as russia has reacted to the much-needed eu sanctions for its aggression in our eastern neighbourhood. the best way we can alleviate the impact of the damage to european businesses and farmers is by facilitating exports and market access elsewhere in the world. market access through procurement is an important accomplishment in the comprehensive economic and trade agreement (ceta) and, by and large, the standards in relation to the planet, privacy and food safety have not been traded away. that is important. more controversial, however, is the investor-state dispute settlement (isds) mechanism. eu member states have no less than 1400 bilateral agreements that include isds, and while the mechanism was sought after by europeans investing in countries where the rule of law does not exist, it is now leading to controversy. in the ceta, i see that some improvements have been realised, such as the inclusion of provisions to increase the impartiality of arbitration judges and to provide more transparency and better protection against frivolous cases, but still the key question of whether or not we need isds between two mature democracies with the rule of law should be answered. this question is relevant not only for the ceta: many of us look at the text of this agreement with the anticipation of finding clues for the much more impactful currently in negotiation. i look forward to learning more about the results of the public consultation, to which an unprecedented number of citizens and stakeholders replied. even though the text of the ceta cannot now be changed, commissioner, i urge the commission to ensure that europeans can engage actively and transparently with you to obtain clarification before having to give their votes.
the ceta agreement is still not concluded. that is good news and it must remain so. there are far too many open questions for the national parliaments to discuss. this agreement gives power to the big companies in europe and canada. they have had ample opportunity to say exactly what they want and the 150,000 reactions of the european people to the isds mechanism are being ignored. these are the people who together elected this parliament and who are also your boss, mr de gucht. it is clear that the people do not want these agreements, and rightly so. there are more and more examples of companies appealing against democratic decision-making. can you guarantee, mr de gucht, that canadian companies like lone pine resources will not force european countries to extract shale gas, as they do in canada? can you guarantee that european countries can decide to freeze nuclear energy without being sued? can you guarantee that our european workers will retain the right to strike and that our collective agreements will remain in force? sixteen european parliaments, including the dutch one, sent you a letter in june saying that both ceta and ttip should remain mixed agreements, so that national parliaments have the final say. these parliaments represent two thirds of the european population. can you guarantee that ceta and ttip are mixed agreements?
i would like to join all the previous speakers in saying that the 20% youth unemployment we have in europe today is unacceptable. we must give the fight against youth unemployment absolute priority over the next five years. the fight against youth unemployment must, however, be carried out first and foremost within the member states themselves. the situation in the various member states is different. that also requires different measures. i am surprised that when the resolution was adopted last july, several groups in this house immediately took the initiative of imposing a legal framework from europe with minimum standards for the youth guarantee. we must set our goals in europe, we must support member states in their measures, but let us also give the member states the opportunity to tailor their own measures to their own situation. if we are to take a serious approach to creating more jobs for young people in the long term, we must implement structural reforms. i believe that one of the structural reforms of the economy and the labour market is the tax burden on labour. in europe we have a tax burden which is significantly higher than the oecd average. if we are serious about creating jobs, we must implement structural, sustainable reforms and we must not continue to put them off.
thousands of people have died in the affected african countries since the ebola outbreak, and research indicates that 70 million people are at risk of infection and one million may have been infected. the outbreak of the virus has dealt a huge blow to these countries, affecting both society and the economy. we in europe must ask ourselves what we could have done better to deal with this virus and what is our response to that? how well do we coordinate that approach and how well do we work together? last week, dutch minister edith schippers authorized the purchase of an ebola vaccine, a step in the right direction. but we're not there yet. what is europe going to do to speed up the development of a vaccine that can be brought to market? are there not enough applications for projects that have a much lower priority? i would ask the commission to look again at its research priorities. because developments in africa show that an ebola vaccine should be our first strong priority in this crisis.

pakistan. so we need more eu leadership to fight jihadist militias, to prevent regional spill-overs and to ensure that the people who flee this violence do not suffer a horrible death by drowning in the mediterranean. we must seek accountability for the perpetrators of violence and end the impunity with which the most horrific crimes are now met. additionally, we should put pressure on those who libya directly impacts us. the refugee flows are enormous, and suggestions such the one as by the dutch prime minister that this is merely an italian problem are inappropriate and no solution. we need european leadership and solutions right now.

madam president, the number of people whose human rights are violated in one way or another is overwhelming. many of them look to us for help and support and we must offer what we can. but underlying the many different forms of violation – from rape and torture to censorship and discrimination – is a wave that is swelling, an increasingly successful challenge to the very principles that define us and that we are defending globally. the universality of human rights itself has perhaps never been as threatened as it is today. the eu is in a great position to lead an unequivocal defence of the universality of human rights but we can only lead by example. it puts us to shame and weakens our credibility when, in europe, viktor orban openly pushes illiberal democracy, when minorities face discrimination, when women do not have equal pay, treatment and opportunities, when technology is used for illegitimate surveillance and hacking in europe or exported to dictators and terrorist organisations which are not curbed, or when the crisis is used as an excuse not to implement corporate social responsibility or trade conditionalities. we can see that the line between internal and external policies is not a sharp one and we are interconnected but if, together, we do not show leadership and strategically leverage our political and economic position, who will defend the universality of human rights?

in writing. - our delegation has just abstained from voting on the report on fiji. we agree that the situation in fiji is a matter of concern. since the coup in 2006, the population has been subject to a lot of repressive measures. human rights are being systematically violated, and in particular the freedoms of expression, assembly and association are under severe pressure. for example, it makes it difficult for workers to organise in a trade union and extremely difficult to form a political party. in view of these circumstances, we think it would be a bad sign if the eu were to conclude an epa with fiji. on the other hand, it is important that the eu is consistent in its policy on international trade and the strengthening of human rights in the world. the eu must be a reliable partner that treats equal countries equally. in this case, fiji is being punished for human rights violations, whereas previous epas have been concluded with countries where the situation is far from ideal. we therefore call for the human rights situation to be examined not only in fiji but in all countries with which the eu is negotiating, as well as in those with which it has already concluded epas.

i would like to make one point about the 2015 budget. what we are seeing is an annual conflict between the countries and the european institutions. it is too tempting to say yes and do no, to make commitments and not pay the payments, especially in difficult times like these. it's an annual conflict, and it's a conflict with no winners. the disadvantages are significant, reinforcing the image of overspending, when in fact spending has decreased since 2013. and, moreover, the european commission is building up a debt, which should not be, in disguise, almost surreptitiously. accounts remain unpaid, jeopardizing investment in knowledge and research. that is not strengthening the european economy, especially in this time of low growth and few jobs. it's a conflict with no winners. i am therefore glad that the european parliament is taking a clear stand and is reversing the cuts in heading 1a. but i also welcome the gesture, if the european parliament does so, of having each party come up with the structural solutions to this annual, entrenched conflict. i hope the commission will take note of this gesture. i hope that this gesture will be seen by the member states. it is seen in the netherlands and is positively received there. let us not be trapped in the annual, baked conflict.

on behalf of the alde group. – madam president, the exceptional provocations and aggression by putin’s russia in our eastern neighbourhood continue to be cause for great concern. at their root is the choice of the ukrainians to seek stronger ties with the eu. choosing rules-based trade and a movement towards an open society will require difficult reforms. the deep and comprehensive trade agreement which is part of the association agreement is set to enter into force on 1 january 2016. meanwhile, preferential trade measures were already adopted in april 2014 as part of the support package for ukraine. since their implementation, trade between the eu and ukraine has increased drastically. ukrainian exports to the eu increased by 25 % in may and june of this year compared to the same period last year and during the first six months of 2014 exports from ukraine to russia fell by 24 %. these figures show the importance of the autonomous trade measures for the ukrainian economy. not extending them would not only seem a political statement but would have a very real economic impact on the country and its people. given the economic asymmetry between the eu and ukraine, the increase in exports from ukraine is economically important for the country but it has relatively little effect on us in the eu. in principle, the autonomous trade measures do not allow illegal products onto the european market. all products must conform to eu standards, but the increase in trade may make it harder to control them. however this should not lead to products coming onto the european market which would otherwise be illegal and which could harm european producers. the regulation before us prolongs the autonomous trade measures and amends the existing regulation to make access by ukraine to the preferential conditions conditional upon respect for democratic principles, human rights and fundamental freedoms, as well as respect for the rule of law. this is also mentioned in the association agreement. it is now important that these conditions are met and that the timeframe is kept. exceptional circumstances call for exceptional measures but the goal remains to see a structural solution.
madam president, i naturally support this arrangement, as do mrs schaake and mrs kallas. this is good for ukraine and we should support ukraine. i find it intolerable, however, that this compromise was reached on the basis of russian blackmail. ukraine has been under enormous pressure. that became clear to me in brussels. that has become clear to me in moscow and in kiev. and finally, in a year and a half, we must not allow that russian pressure to be rewarded again. that can't happen. i also agree with those who say that our agricultural sector must not reap the sour fruits. animal welfare, public health, these are the kinds of things that obviously should not be submitted. it is necessary to consider whether the agricultural sector can be given some compensation because it too is suffering from a russian embargo. but again, we have to help ukraine and that is why we are fully in favour of this arrangement.
member of the commission. - madam president, the eu should definitely continue to support the comprehensive peace process in ukraine, respecting the country’s territorial integrity and its right to decide on its destiny. we must stand shoulder to shoulder with ukraine in the face of the extremely difficult political and territorial challenges it faces but also in its process of reforms and economic modernisation. parliament’s support in these efforts is vital. delays in the extension of trade preferences should indeed be avoided as this could have an impact on economic operators’ decisions to import goods from ukraine. even retroactive application risks not fully repairing interferences with trade flows and would be cumbersome not only for the economic operators but also for our competent national customs authorities. therefore i urge parliament to endorse the commission’s proposal without amendments. the council should then do its part so that the legislative act can be signed into law and enter into force as soon as possible.
in writing. today we are debating the abolition of customs duties on ukrainian goods. or for the sake of completeness: the proposal for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council amending regulation (eu) no 374/2014 on the reduction or elimination of customs duties on goods originating in ukraine. we all know that the intensification of trade relations with ukraine - and thus the increase in european influence in ukraine - can have major geopolitical consequences. so i will not go into that now. but i see another problem: the eu has banned the use of leaf litter and the sale of leaf litter eggs, while ukraine simply allows animal-friendly leaf litter. and what does it show? this proposal simply allows animal products from ukraine, regardless of the living conditions of the animals. how can the eu say with dry eyes that it will end animal-friendly production methods if we simply allow these products through the back door? thanks to the european commission, which has already authorised a major ukrainian egg producer to export to europe, hatchery eggs will soon be back in our shops. i don't understand why you're doing this! i believe that international security, human rights, democracy and the respectful treatment of animals are more important than short-sighted trade policy. that is why i shall vote against this report.
mr president, i would like to say to mr juncker that alde will support his european commission, but mr juncker, let us be clear, we support you in the vote today; that does not mean that you will have a blank cheque for the next five years. our support is not automatic and you will have to work very hard to get our support for every single proposal that you put forward. but you will get our support for anything taking europe forward, making it more democratic, more efficient and better able to act and to face the challenges and the crises of today. we need to get out of the economic crisis, but europe also needs to take up its responsibility on the world stage. for that we need a strong european commission. alde voters went to the ballot box for a stronger, better, different and reformed european union. we want better regulation, yes. but better regulation in our view does not mean dismantling the achievements of the european union. the voters on the right side of the house – there are very few people left there – they also want a change; they want to dissolve the european union. that is not the solution proposed by alde, but we do want europe to move, and i would be very worried to see a so-called große koalition, which seems to be a paralysing embrace leading to stagnation and status quo. europe needs to move. we have talked a lot about the economy, about foreign affairs, about an energy union – lots of concrete things – but i have heard very little about values. values are not a side issue. they are actually the essence, the core, of european integration and if we want to operate as a political union now we need a community of citizens based on shared values. so i expect initiatives for stronger instruments to promote and protect democracy, the rule of law and fundamental rights. but we also expect better enforcement if these values are under pressure in one of the member states and i am looking in particular to mr timmermans, your, let us say, prime minister, who will be in charge of this task. so mr juncker, today, you have our support and you will find us at your side – if you take europe forward.
the citizens want peace and prosperity, but what does this commission want? taking away citizens' pensions and taxing savings. we must not have more european integration, but less. the citizens want peace and prosperity, but what does this commission want? the eu's imperialism knows no bounds at the expense of our security. we need more countries out of the eu, not in the eu. the citizens want peace and prosperity, but what does this commission want? eu travel agencies in north africa to bring in even more money seekers with their barbaric islamic culture. murder, rape and violence have become the norm in our cities thanks to open borders. we do not need external borders, but national borders, as also called for in un resolution 2178. the european union has failed, but this commission continues down the road to nowhere. i ask you: step up and make way for people who will start the dismantling.
the new commission certainly has some positive aspects. i am thinking of the pressure of brussels, which many citizens and businesses are tired of. mr juncker is playing a good game here by giving his first vice-president the task of putting the knife in. it is also right, mr president, that the minister who has for years violated the rules of the stability and growth pact should be restrained by two colleagues. yes, i am referring to the french commissioner. and in the juncker team i meet a few star players like mr timmermans, like the commissioner for agriculture and like the british commissioner. those are positive points. however, mr president, i cannot support the juncker team and i shall abstain because mr juncker's programme is 'more europe, more monetary union and a firm commitment to the euro'. moreover, mr president, some of the commissioners on the team are simply too weak. i have already mentioned the french commissioner, but i also think that the matter of the transport commissioner was not handled wisely. that is why the dutch delegation in the ecr group abstained.
mr president, i would like to welcome mr juncker and his team. i have one minute and will only make one point. the new commission, the team juncker, can push europe in the right direction, towards the hearts and minds of europeans – our voters – by making europe more social, green and transparent. but to do that and push in the right direction europe must get out of the muddling-through scenario of low growth and high unemployment. to do that, we need to get the european economy back on track. increasing private and public investment is key – raising demand in the short term and improving the supply side in the longer term. that is why so many social democrats find the investment plan of eur 300 billion crucial for the economy and for europe. we do not doubt the good intentions of the commission, but we are really disappointed by the lack of detail thus far. only if the investment plans turn out to be real and substantial can the commission continue to count on our cooperation. good luck!
president. - the commission's last chance report is a bit melodramatic, but i think the analysis is correct. europe suffers from a lack of confidence among many citizens. and it is up to this european commission, together with the council and the european parliament, to restore that trust by delivering and by focusing on core tasks. the cda in the european parliament also strongly supports the first vice-president who is responsible for monitoring this focus. as far as we're concerned, those core tasks are: a fair internal market, the securing of raw materials, food and energy, and that in a sustainable way, migration and the much stronger speaking more one voice in the world. but the real challenge ... i am quoting jean-luc dehaene, freely, but i am quoting him and i want to pay tribute to him here, because unfortunately he was lost earlier this year. jean-luc dehaene used to say: "the big question for europe is: what is the future of europe? it will be a bock empire, it will be an open-air museum, or will we succeed in maintaining the competitiveness of our economy?" if you know that european hands are never going to be as cheap as chinese hands, europe will have to be the continent that keeps reinventing itself through smart and sustainable solutions that keep those european hands working. so growth and jobs through smart and sustainable solutions, and - i say to mr tang - not by saddling the coming generations with towering debts. i do not want to be part of the generation of politicians who, in a difficult time, are destroying what my grandparents' generation, my parents' generation, have built in europe. it is precisely now, when things are getting difficult, that europe is worth fighting for. that's what i'm gonna do for the next five years. and i expect no less from you. i wish you the very best of luck.
in writing. - i voted against the eu budget for 2015 because it is not future-proof. there is no investment in animals, nature and the environment. the eu has only one objective: growth! economic growth! almost half of the budget is devoted to agricultural subsidies, regardless of the impact on animals, people, nature or the environment. there is little investment in biodiversity. instead of investing in sustainable agriculture, with plant-based food production at its core, the eu subsidises intensive livestock farming and maintains the transport of live animals for millennia. the european parliament approves this without hesitation; the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat fraud. take bullfighting, for example. a vast majority of europeans do not want bullfights to be sponsored with their taxpayers' money. however, there was no qualified majority in parliament to actually want to abolish subsidies for bullfighting, whereas the political will did appear a little later (amendment 12, report on the council's position on the draft general budget of the european union for the financial year 2015). these differences are inexplicable and further fuel citizens' mistrust of the european union.
in writing. - france and italy must abide by eurozone agreements! the european union has made agreements to improve the structure and functioning of the eurozone. on wednesday, october 22nd, we'll discuss one of those appointments: the european semester for the coordination of economic policies. at the same time, we see that some countries are trying to bend the rules that have been agreed. the new budgets of france and italy show that these countries refuse to comply with the commitments of the stability and growth pact. if france and italy do not comply with the stability and growth pact, we will be back to the situation of 2003. then germany and france ignored the rules of the sgp and thus laid the foundations for the current euro crisis. it is therefore of the utmost importance that the rules of the sgp are respected. france and italy must comply with the rules they themselves agreed to a few years ago.
mr president, in the last weeks we have witnessed a humanitarian crisis unfolding in kobane. around 200 000 people have escaped the terror of islamic state, seeking refuge in turkey. as has been said already by many previous speakers, kobane has become the symbol of the fight against this terrorist organisation, which poses a threat not only to the security of syrians, iraqis and countries in the region, but a threat to all of us. that is also the reason why we have to be united in our fight against is. the situation in kobane has already deeply affected the internal situation in turkey. we witness different groups within turkish society attacking each other and putting extra pressure on the settlement process. it is of crucial importance that the peace process remain on the table and that all sides continue with the road map that is to be set up. it is our moral obligation to support the kurds fighting off is and defending their city in kobane. what turkey has done in terms of taking up a huge number of refugees from syria is to be commended, and i also welcome the latest statements that not only humanitarian aid can pass through the border from turkey to syria but also the kurds from iraq will be allowed to reinforce the kurds in kobane. we have to jointly ensure that we will not allow islamic state this important symbolic victory, as it would also certainly set back the morale of the iraqi kurds. not neglecting the situation in kobane, we must also take into account the overall situation. the threat of islamic state has certainly not diminished, also in iraq, where it has even become bigger in some parts of the country. last but not least, let us not forget that a large part of the funding of this terrorist organisation comes from the illicit sale of oil and goods. we have to ascertain that eu member states are in no case involved in the illicit trade and all channels used by is to sell this oil need to be closed off.

president. - the next item is the report (a4-0179/97) by mr pimenta, on behalf of the committee on the environment, public health and consumer protection, on the proposal from the commission to the council (com (97) 0406 - c4-0179/97). it was almost unanimously decided to hold this debate today on asia bibi. our proposal has broad support. we are talking about asia bibi. we're talking about this blasphemy law. we're talking about the death penalty that can be imposed for alleged insult to the prophet muhammad. the commission has already made a number of proposals. now stop that death sentence against asia bibi, stop that blasphemy law, end the thousands of situations that are similar to asia bibi's. this parliament was crystal clear last monday. and the high representative must now quickly come up with any means of preventing the execution of that death sentence. i am thinking here of trade sanctions based on the agreements we have with pakistan. now is the time to take action, and not just for her. we know there are thousands of other "asia bibi's" in the same circumstances. the time for talking to pakistan is over. it is time for action, for action. not words, but actions. stop the blasphemy laws! free asia bibi now! our prayers are with her.

president. - i fully agree with some of the previous speakers who have spoken on the distressing case of asia bibi. it is a matter of concern to me that, as in the previous debate on kobani, the european commission, in the capacity of mrs ashton, is absent, despite the fact that we are dealing with substantial and important matters. what can we do as europe? can we allow this kind of practice to take place in a country with which we have a partnership agreement, article 1 of which says that it is based on shared values, on shared values of fundamental freedoms, democracy and the rule of law, when we can see with our own eyes that democracy and the rule of law do not exist there at the moment? can we allow this to happen in a country which benefits fully from the general system of tariff preferences which we as europe offer because of the progress being made? are the european commission and the european council prepared to use that cooperation agreement and that system of preferential conditions as a means of pressure to put pressure on the pakistani government to commit itself to asia bibi and also to do much more in the general area of the adaptation of the blasphemy laws?
mr president, earlier this year a pakistani christian told us that many muslims in his country consider christians to be 'unclean' and therefore do not want to live with them in a village or neighbourhood. he said it was a profound thing. i quote him: "they are wounding us mentally". mr president, it was precisely this social exclusion, on religious grounds, 'christian impurity', that led to an exchange of words between the pakistani christian woman asia bibi and some of her fellow villagers in 2009. what followed was a pure violation of the law under the invocation of a poisonous blasphemy law, and the death sentence was signed against asia bibi. until today! what does asia bibi expect from western christians? i quote her word for word: "prayer and a call to their governments to protect persecuted pakistani christians". that is why i am today making a strong appeal to the 28 member states of the european union: the european union is also calling for the release of the three men who were killed in the attack on the un headquarters in kabul. with this pakistani self-purification, asia bibi will surely be released and pakistani christians will surely become free citizens!
thank you, mr president. i would also like to thank mrs malmström for her presence here. we have missed her colleague, mrs ashton, in the last three debates. i thank you very much for the respect you do show to parliament by being here and discussing these important matters with us. we adopted a resolution just before the summer recess, stressing the urgency of the fight against isis in iraq and syria. since we adopted that resolution, more than a thousand jihadists have left europe for syria and iraq. we need a coordinated european approach to tackle this problem. as jean-claude juncker rightly said in his speech this morning: the european union is not a party to the conflict, but a party to the peace process. the 2013 council adopted four priorities for such a coordinated approach: prevention, information exchange, criminal law and cooperation with third countries. i wonder now: how far has this approach progressed and what concrete results have been achieved? a specific question i would like to ask is about the europol focal point for travellers. the purpose of this is to exchange and analyse information on suspects who may wish to travel abroad. are all countries now active in this network? if not, do you think that participation should be made compulsory in order to ensure the most efficient approach possible? point two: does this system apply to returning jihadists? can we also monitor people returning from syria and iraq through a european register that all countries participate in? finally, i would like to say to my colleague, frank engel: we need to fight isis in syria and iraq, but we also need to pay close attention to the grounds on which young people are being radicalized in our own member states and the ways in which organisations are being given the space to facilitate and organise travel to syria and iraq from our own european countries. the member states must also take responsibility and take important steps in this respect.
president. - the next item is the report (a4-0173/97) by mr pimenta, on behalf of the committee on legal affairs and citizens' rights, on the proposal for a council regulation (ec) amending regulation (eec) no 1408/71 on the application of social security schemes to employed persons and their families moving within the community (com (97) 0406 - c4-0173/97). estimates vary from 150,000 to 450,000 people. the european union is responsible for this. the european union facilitates smugglers and their dangerous journeys in old boats by providing frontex not as a border guard but as a shuttle service. immigrants are being picked up at sea and brought to european shores instead of being returned to their countries of origin. once on european soil, they disappear, only to reappear kilometres away within the schengen area, for example in the netherlands, and apply for asylum. operation mos maiorum is therefore moping with the faucet open. this operation demonstrates all the more the failure of this policy and the need to return the competence for asylum and immigration to the member states. this is the only way that anything can really be done to control this constant flow of immigration. the member states should regain their competence over their own borders and set up reception centres in the region.
mr president, it is of course a good thing that human trafficking is being tackled. right now, criminals outside are expressing the desperation of migrants. but i fear that in this kind of action not only the smugglers are being targeted, but also the undocumented migrants are being branded and treated as criminals. the operation mos maiorum therefore raises many questions. for example, i have great concerns about political accountability, transparency and democratic control, and in addition, about the risk of discrimination and racial profiling. it is unacceptable that this kind of joint police operation is not subject to any democratic control by the european parliament or by the national parliaments, and that such actions raise many questions. like this: what happens to the migrants who are detained without the proper papers? what happens to the intelligence gathered during previous operations? and does this not lead to repressive actions that in turn lead to a greater sense of hostility towards migrants in society? the first step towards transparency has been taken by holding this debate here tonight and i hope that the council will come up with concrete answers.
author. - madam president, putin’s aggression towards his neighbours requires urgent attention, but must never overshadow the human rights situation in russia itself. civil society, journalists and opposition figures are all repressed and intimidated and the russian people live in an unfree free society, although propaganda suggests otherwise. laws systematically undermine freedoms and give authorities greater unchecked powers. the closing of memorial is more than symbolic. its founder, andrei sakharov, inspired the name of our human rights award which was given to memorial in 2009. we urge the scheduled law suit against them of 13 november to be recalled and to revoke all conspiracy-theory-driven foreign agent laws against ngos. the government of the russian federation should at the very least adhere to agreements it has itself made in the context of the council of europe, the osce, the european court of human rights and, of course, its own constitution. we see a clear difference between ill-guided kremlin policies and the population of russia, and we support their aspiration for a just, democratic and free russia.
